[头颈癌的放射治疗:2026 年 ASCO 年会亮点]。

[Radiotherapy of head and neck cancer : Highlights from the 2026 ASCO Annual Meeting].
作者： Sören Schnellhardt, Silke Tribius, Maximilian Linxweiler, Christoph Süß, Markus Hecht
摘要中文翻译在2026年美国临床肿瘤学会(ASCO)年会上,多项研究聚焦于头颈鳞状细胞癌(HNSCC)的放射治疗。III期AREST试验表明,对具有中危特征的早期口腔癌进行辅助放疗可以降低局部复发的风险。JCOG1008 试验的事后分析发现,即使辅助放化疗开始时间晚于推荐时间,局部复发率也没有增加。联合放射免疫治疗 (RIT) 也是今年的一个主要话题:在 NIVOPOSTOP 试验的亚组分析中,纳武单抗在广泛颈清扫患者中维持了无病生存 (DFS) 益处,这削弱了经常强调的区域淋巴结与 免疫应答 的机械相关性。在 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 阳性口咽癌的 治疗 中,RIT 的结果是相互矛盾的:评估放疗联合 durvalumab 的 II 期 CCTG HN.9 试验未能达到其疗效终点,而联合派姆单抗确定性 RIT 的 II 期 KEYCHAIN 试验表明,与基于顺铂的放化疗相比,无进展生存期 (PFS) 显着改善。此外,对 KEYNOTE-412 试验的综合阳性评分 (CPS) ≥ 10 人群进行的亚组分析强调了结合放疗和免疫治疗时基于生物标志物的患者选择的重要性。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 在头颈癌中的作用,特别是与放射治疗及其对局部复发和生存结果的影响有关。核心问题是口咽癌等 HPV 相关疾病是否受益于特定的放射治疗方法,包括联合放射免疫治疗。
证据基础与方法
该审查基于 2026 年 ASCO 年会的结果,包括 III 期 AREST 试验、JCOG1008 试验的事后分析以及 NIVOPOSTOP 和 KEYNOTE-412 试验的亚组分析。这些研究的选择是基于它们与 HPV 相关疾病的相关性以及它们对放射治疗结果的关注。该综述没有明确描述荟萃分析或共识过程,但总结了这些试验的主要结果。
共识与结论
该综述强调,对具有中等风险特征的早期口腔癌进行辅助放疗可以减少局部复发。此外,它指出,纳武单抗在联合放射​​免疫治疗 (RIT) 中的 DFS 益处在广泛颈清扫患者中得以维持,这表明区域淋巴结对于 免疫应答 可能并不像之前认为的那么重要。然而,RIT 在 HPV 阳性口咽癌中的结果是相互矛盾的,CCTG HN.9 试验未能达到其疗效终点,而 KEYCHAIN 试验显示与基于顺铂的放化疗相比,无进展生存期 (PFS) 显着改善。
争议与缺口
关于联合放射免疫治疗 (RIT) 对 HPV 阳性口咽癌的疗效存在争议,因为 CCTG HN.9 试验未达到其疗效终点,而 KEYCHAIN 试验显示 PFS 显着改善。此外,该评论指出,KEYNOTE-412 试验的亚组分析强调了在放射治疗和免疫治疗相结合时基于生物标志物的患者选择的重要性,这表明在理解如何为此类 治疗 最佳选择患者方面存在差距。
科研与实践启示
该综述表明,未来的研究应重点关注针对 HPV 相关癌症的联合放疗和免疫治疗方法,完善基于生物标志物的患者选择。它还强调需要进一步研究来解决 RIT 对 HPV 阳性口咽癌疗效的相互矛盾的结果。在实践中,这些发现可能为 HPV 相关头颈癌患者的 治疗 策略提供参考,特别是那些具有中等风险特征或广泛颈清扫术的患者。

[Radiotherapy of head and neck cancer : Highlights from the 2026 ASCO Annual Meeting].

Authors: Sören Schnellhardt, Silke Tribius, Maximilian Linxweiler, Christoph Süß, Markus Hecht
Original abstract (English)
At the 2026 American Society of Clinical Oncology (ASCO) Annual Meeting, several studies focused on radiotherapy of head and neck squamous cell carcinoma (HNSCC). The phase III AREST trial showed that adjuvant radiotherapy for early oral cavity cancer with intermediate-risk features can reduce the risk of locoregional recurrence. A post hoc analysis of the JCOG1008 trial found no increased locoregional recurrence rate even when adjuvant chemoradiotherapy was initiated later than recommended. Combined radioimmunotherapy (RIT) was also a major topic this year: in a subgroup analysis of the NIVOPOSTOP trial, the disease-free survival (DFS) benefit of nivolumab was maintained in patients with extensive neck dissection, which weakens the frequently highlighted mechanistic relevance of regional lymph nodes for the immune response. In the treatment of human papillomavirus (HPV)-positive oropharyngeal cancer, results for RIT were conflicting: the phase II CCTG HN.9 trial evaluating radiotherapy combined with durvalumab failed to meet its efficacy endpoint, whereas the phase II KEYCHAIN trial of definitive RIT with pembrolizumab demonstrated a significant improvement in progression-free survival (PFS) compared with cisplatin-based chemoradiotherapy. In addition, a subgroup analysis of the combined positive score (CPS) ≥ 10 population of the KEYNOTE-412 trial underscored the importance of biomarker-based patient selection when combining radiotherapy and immunotherapy.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the role of human papillomaviruses (HPV) in the context of head and neck cancer, particularly in relation to radiotherapy and its impact on locoregional recurrence and survival outcomes. The central question is whether HPV-associated diseases, such as oropharyngeal cancer, benefit from specific radiotherapy approaches, including combined radioimmunotherapy.
Evidence base and method
The review is based on findings from the 2026 ASCO Annual Meeting, including the phase III AREST trial, the post hoc analysis of the JCOG1008 trial, and subgroup analyses from the NIVOPOSTOP and KEYNOTE-412 trials. These studies were selected based on their relevance to HPV-associated diseases and their focus on radiotherapy outcomes. The review does not explicitly describe a meta-analysis or consensus process, but it summarizes key results from these trials.
Consensus and conclusions
The review highlights that adjuvant radiotherapy for early oral cavity cancer with intermediate-risk features can reduce locoregional recurrence. Additionally, it notes that the DFS benefit of nivolumab in combined radioimmunotherapy (RIT) is maintained in patients with extensive neck dissection, suggesting that regional lymph nodes may not be as critical for immune response as previously thought. However, the results for RIT in HPV-positive oropharyngeal cancer are conflicting, with the CCTG HN.9 trial failing to meet its efficacy endpoint and the KEYCHAIN trial showing significant improvement in progression-free survival (PFS) compared with cisplatin-based chemoradiotherapy.
Controversies and gaps
There is controversy regarding the efficacy of combined radioimmunotherapy (RIT) in HPV-positive oropharyngeal cancer, as the CCTG HN.9 trial did not meet its efficacy endpoint while the KEYCHAIN trial showed significant improvement in PFS. Additionally, the review notes that subgroup analyses of the KEYNOTE-412 trial emphasize the importance of biomarker-based patient selection when combining radiotherapy and immunotherapy, indicating a gap in understanding how to optimally select patients for such treatments.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests that future research should focus on refining biomarker-based patient selection for combined radiotherapy and immunotherapy approaches in HPV-associated cancers. It also highlights the need for further studies to resolve the conflicting results on the efficacy of RIT in HPV-positive oropharyngeal cancer. In practice, these findings may inform treatment strategies for patients with HPV-associated head and neck cancers, particularly those with intermediate-risk features or extensive neck dissection.

巴基斯坦大学生对 宫颈癌 预防和 HPV 疫苗接种 的认识和了解:一项横断面研究

Awareness and knowledge of cervical cancer prevention and HPV vaccination among university students in Pakistan: a cross-sectional study
作者： Marie Wieneke, Mathias Bonk, Meike Schild-Suhren, Sumbal Shabaz
摘要中文翻译摘要 背景 宫颈癌 是全球女性癌症相关死亡的主要原因之一,尽管在很大程度上是可以预防的。在没有有组织的 疫苗接种 和筛查计划的情况下,其负担仍然很高。对 宫颈癌 预防和 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 疫苗接种 的了解和认识可能会影响对预防措施的接受。本研究旨在评估巴基斯坦拉合尔大学生对 宫颈癌 预防和 HPV 疫苗接种 的了解和认识,并确定他们健康信息的主要来源。方法 对巴基斯坦拉合尔大学医学相关课程的学生进行了结构化、定量的在线英语调查。结果共有163名学生参加。对 宫颈癌、HPV 和预防措施的了解程度为低至中等。只有 69 名参与者 (42%) 认为 HPV 感染是一个危险因素,56 名参与者 (34%) 不知道可以通过巴氏涂片筛查来预防 宫颈癌。在女性受访者中,只有 10 人(12%)认为自己面临风险。对 HPV 传播 和 疫苗接种 的了解有限,其目的、时机和益处尚不确定。接受 HPV 疫苗 的意愿中等 (62%)。健康信息的主要来源是教育机构、社交媒体和医疗保健专业人员,其中医疗保健专业人员和教育机构被认为是最值得信赖的。结论 尽管人群受教育程度相对较高,但对 宫颈癌 预防和 HPV 疫苗接种 的了解仍然不足。有针对性的教育干预措施和全面的公共卫生策略,包括文化敏感的沟通以及获得 疫苗接种 和筛查计划,对于增强预防效果至关重要。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
尽管在很大程度上是可以预防的,但 宫颈癌 是导致全球女性癌症相关死亡的主要原因之一。本研究旨在评估巴基斯坦拉合尔大学生对 宫颈癌 预防和 HPV 疫苗接种 的了解和认识,并确定他们健康信息的主要来源。

对巴基斯坦拉合尔大学医学相关课程的学生进行了一项结构化、定量的在线英语调查。健康信息的主要来源是教育机构、社交媒体和医疗保健专业人员,其中医疗保健专业人员和教育机构被认为是最值得信赖的。

对 宫颈癌、HPV 和预防措施的了解程度为低至中等。对 HPV 传播 和 疫苗接种 的了解有限,其目的、时机和益处尚不确定。接受 HPV 疫苗 的意愿中等 (62%)。

在没有有组织的 疫苗接种 和筛查计划的情况下,其负担仍然很高。所提供的证据并未报告教育干预措施的有效性或文化因素对 疫苗 接受度的影响。
科研与实践启示
有针对性的教育干预措施和全面的公共卫生策略,包括文化敏感的沟通以及获得 疫苗接种 和筛查计划,对于增强预防效果至关重要。

Awareness and knowledge of cervical cancer prevention and HPV vaccination among university students in Pakistan: a cross-sectional study

Authors: Marie Wieneke, Mathias Bonk, Meike Schild-Suhren, Sumbal Shabaz
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background Cervical cancer is one of the leading causes of cancer-related deaths among women worldwide, despite being largely preventable. Its burden remains high in settings without organized vaccination and screening programs. Knowledge and awareness of cervical cancer prevention and human papillomavirus (HPV) vaccination may influence the acceptance of preventive measures. This study aimed to assess knowledge and awareness of cervical cancer prevention and HPV vaccination among university students in Lahore, Pakistan, and to identify their main sources of health information. Methods A structured, quantitative, online survey in English was administered to students enrolled in medical-related programs at the University of Lahore, Pakistan. Results A total of 163 students participated. Knowledge of cervical cancer, HPV, and preventive measures was low to moderate. Only 69 participants (42%) recognized HPV infection as a risk factor, and 56 (34%) were unaware that cervical cancer can be prevented through Pap smear screening. Among female respondents, only 10 (12%) perceived themselves to be at risk. Knowledge of HPV transmission and vaccination was limited, with uncertainty regarding the purpose, timing, and benefits. Willingness to receive the HPV vaccine was moderate (62%). The main sources of health information were educational institutions, social media, and healthcare professionals, with healthcare professionals and educational institutions reported as the most trusted. Conclusions Despite a relatively educated population, knowledge of cervical cancer prevention and HPV vaccination remains insufficient. Targeted educational interventions and comprehensive public health strategies, including culturally sensitive communication and access to vaccination and screening programs, are essential to enhance preventive outcomes.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Cervical cancer is one of the leading causes of cancer-related deaths among women worldwide, despite being largely preventable. This study aimed to assess knowledge and awareness of cervical cancer prevention and HPV vaccination among university students in Lahore, Pakistan, and to identify their main sources of health information.
Evidence base and method
A structured, quantitative, online survey in English was administered to students enrolled in medical-related programs at the University of Lahore, Pakistan. The main sources of health information were educational institutions, social media, and healthcare professionals, with healthcare professionals and educational institutions reported as the most trusted.
Consensus and conclusions
Knowledge of cervical cancer, HPV, and preventive measures was low to moderate. Knowledge of HPV transmission and vaccination was limited, with uncertainty regarding the purpose, timing, and benefits. Willingness to receive the HPV vaccine was moderate (62%).
Controversies and gaps
Its burden remains high in settings without organized vaccination and screening programs. The supplied evidence does not report on the effectiveness of educational interventions or the impact of cultural factors on vaccine acceptance.
Research and practice implications
Targeted educational interventions and comprehensive public health strategies, including culturally sensitive communication and access to vaccination and screening programs, are essential to enhance preventive outcomes.

疫苗 癌症:转化科学评论。

Vaccines for Cancer: A Translational Science Review.
作者： Mary L Disis, Brie M N Chun, Kiranjit K Dhillon, John B Liao, Natasha Hunter, Denise L Cecil
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述的重点是癌症 疫苗,特别是那些针对 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 的药物,重点是它们在治疗和预防 HPV 相关恶性肿瘤(如宫颈癌、肛门癌和口咽癌)中的作用。核心问题是癌症疫苗能否有效治疗和预防这些特定的HPV相关疾病。
证据基础与方法
该综述是对癌症 疫苗 的系统分析,包括其发展、机制和临床应用。它引用了 疫苗 的研究,例如 Sipuleucel-T 和 talimogene laherparepvec,这些研究评估了它们在治疗特定癌症方面的功效。该综述还讨论了识别免疫原性肿瘤蛋白和开发安全的 疫苗 递送技术的总体进展。
共识与结论
癌症 疫苗 已显示出治疗某些 HPV 相关癌症的功效,例如黑色素瘤、前列腺癌和膀胱癌。这些 疫苗 刺激 T 和 B 淋巴细胞产生特异性且持久的 免疫应答 抗癌药物。此外,正在对它们进行癌症复发一级预防和预防癌症的评估。
争议与缺口
该评论强调,虽然一些 疫苗 已被证明有效,但其用于其他癌症的证据仍然有限。在理解这些 疫苗 的长期影响及其在不同癌症类型中更广泛应用的潜力方面也存在差距。
科研与实践启示
需要进一步的研究来评估癌症 疫苗 在预防癌症复发和一级预防方面的有效性。临床试验应侧重于将这些 疫苗 的使用范围扩大到其他 HPV 相关癌症,并改善其与现有 治疗(如化疗和免疫疗法)的整合。

Vaccines for Cancer: A Translational Science Review.

Authors: Mary L Disis, Brie M N Chun, Kiranjit K Dhillon, John B Liao, Natasha Hunter, Denise L Cecil
Original abstract (English)
IMPORTANCE: Advances in tumor immunology and vaccine technology are reshaping cancer vaccine development after decades of limited clinical success. More than 2000 immunogenic tumor proteins or antigens have been identified, the types of immune responses required for tumor eradication have been defined, and safe and effective vaccine delivery technologies have been developed. This progress has renewed clinical investigation of cancer vaccines for multiple types of cancer. OBSERVATIONS: Vaccines, such as those against human papillomavirus and hepatitis B, are composed of (1) an antigen target, (2) an adjuvant to activate the immune system, and (3) a vaccine delivery vehicle. Antigens can be derived from tumor mutations termed neoantigens, or cancer-associated nonmutated proteins, which are normal human proteins that are abnormally expressed in cancer. The tumor can also act as an antigen source, such as when immunization occurs with whole tumor cells or by directly injecting an immunogenic substance into the tumor to elicit an adaptive immune response. T and B lymphocytes, cells of the adaptive immune system, are stimulated by cancer vaccines and can provide a highly specific and durable immune response directed against cancer. Vaccines that elicit adaptive immunity are approved for cancer treatment. Sipuleucel-T, a vaccine against the antigen prostatic acid phosphatase, reduced all-cause mortality compared with placebo in men with metastatic castrate-resistant prostate cancer (32% vs 23%; hazard ratio, 0.78 [95% CI, 0.61-0.98]; P = .03). Compared with granulocyte-macrophage colony-stimulating factor, a systemic immune stimulant, talimogene laherparepvec increased rates of a 6-month or longer complete or partial response, defined as the disappearance of all treated disease and at least a 50% decrease in the sum of the products of perpendicular diameters of the measurable lesion with no new lesions, respectively, when injected into advanced melanoma lesions (16.3% [95% CI, 12.1%-20.5%] vs 2.1% [95% CI, 0%-4.5%]; odds ratio, 8.9; P < .001). Most cancer vaccines have mild adverse effects, such as fatigue, fever, and injection site reactions. Combining cancer vaccines with chemotherapy or immunotherapy can increase the magnitude of the immune response and potentially result in greater reduction in cancer size. Combining the personalized mutation-based messenger RNA (mRNA) vaccine (mRNA-4157) with the immune checkpoint inhibitor pembrolizumab for resected high-risk stage IIIB to IV cutaneous melanoma improved relapse-free survival compared with pembrolizumab alone (76.6% vs 60%; hazard ratio, 0.56 [95% CI, 0.31-1.02]; P = .05). Cancer vaccines are undergoing evaluation in clinical trials to prevent cancer recurrence and for primary cancer prevention. CONCLUSIONS AND RELEVANCE: Cancer vaccines can treat several types of malignant neoplasms, including melanoma, prostate cancer, and bladder cancer. Adverse effects associated with cancer vaccines are generally mild and of short duration, promoting eventual integration of vaccination into treatment regimens with chemotherapy and immunotherapy. Cancer vaccines are increasingly undergoing evaluation for primary prevention.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on cancer vaccines, particularly those targeting human papillomaviruses (HPV), with an emphasis on their role in treating and preventing HPV-associated malignancies such as cervical, anal, and oropharyngeal cancers. The central question is whether cancer vaccines can effectively treat and prevent these specific HPV-related diseases.
Evidence base and method
The review is a systematic analysis of cancer vaccines, including their development, mechanisms, and clinical applications. It references studies on vaccines like Sipuleucel-T and talimogene laherparepvec, which are evaluated for their efficacy in treating specific cancers. The review also discusses the general progress in identifying immunogenic tumor proteins and developing safe vaccine delivery technologies.
Consensus and conclusions
Cancer vaccines have shown efficacy in treating certain HPV-associated cancers, such as melanoma, prostate cancer, and bladder cancer. These vaccines stimulate T and B lymphocytes to produce a specific and durable immune response against cancer. Additionally, they are being evaluated for primary prevention of cancer recurrence and for preventing cancer in the first place.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights that while some vaccines have demonstrated effectiveness, the evidence for their use in other cancers remains limited. There are also gaps in understanding the long-term effects of these vaccines and their potential for broader application across different cancer types.
Research and practice implications
Further research is needed to evaluate the effectiveness of cancer vaccines in preventing cancer recurrence and for primary prevention. Clinical trials should focus on expanding the use of these vaccines to other HPV-associated cancers and improving their integration with existing treatments like chemotherapy and immunotherapy.

🚨 突发动态与新闻 / Health News

Published: 2026-10-26  |  Publisher: Bioengineer.org
B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

高危型HPV 在无症状的乌干达农村妇女中很常见 - Bioengineer.org

High-risk HPV common among asymptomatic rural Ugandan women - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报2026 年 10 月 26 日至 30 日在乌干达农村地区进行的一项横断面研究,检查了 7,126 名 30-49 岁无症状女性的高危 人乳头瘤病毒 (hrHPV) 的 患病率。研究发现,25.3% 的参与者 hrHPV 检测呈阳性,其中 HPV16 和 HPV18 群体之外的感染很常见。在1,800名hrHPV阳性女性中,1,422名拥有完整的基因型数据,显示HPV31/33/35/52/58是最常见的基因型(22.8%),其次是多种基因型(27.9%)。在接受醋酸(VIA)目视检查的 1,121 名 hrHPV 阳性女性中,159 名(14.2%)检查呈阳性,8 名(0.7%)被确定为 宫颈癌。然而,研究发现 hrHPV 基因型与 VIA 结果之间没有统计学上的显着关系。作者强调需要进一步研究这些基因型在宫颈癌发生中的作用,以及现有的 疫苗 对观察到的更广泛的 hrHPV 类型的有效性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 10 月 26 日至 30 日在乌干达农村地区进行的一项横断面研究发现,7,126 名 30-49 岁的女性中有 1,800 名 高危型HPV (hrHPV) 检测呈阳性。
地点与对象
乌干达农村地区,特别是 7,126 名 30-49 岁无症状女性。
事件
一项横断面研究发现,在无症状的乌干达农村妇女中,患病率 (hrHPV) 的 患病率 检测结果呈高水平,其中 25.3% 的检测结果呈阳性。研究还指出,HPV16 和 HPV18 群体之外的感染很常见,并且 hrHPV 基因型与 VIA 结果之间没有发现统计学上的显着关系。
规模、影响与风险
在 1,800 名 hrHPV 阳性参与者中,1,422 名拥有完整的基因型数据。最大的基因型组是HPV31/33/35/52/58(22.8%),其次是多基因型(27.9%)和其他组。在接受 VIA 的 1,121 名 hrHPV 阳性女性中,159 名(14.2%)检查结果呈阳性,8 名(0.7%)被确定为 宫颈癌。该研究并未建立基因型类别与 VIA 结果之间的统计关联。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究没有进行组织病理学分析,因此临床观察缺乏组织学特征。hrHPV 阳性参与者被邀请进行视觉评估和 治疗,无论是否有 VIA。作者建议进一步研究特定基因型在宫颈癌发生中的作用以及 疫苗 针对发现的更广泛的 hrHPV 基因型的功效。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

High-risk HPV common among asymptomatic rural Ugandan women - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A cross-sectional study conducted in rural Uganda between October 26 and October 30, 2026, examined the prevalence of high-risk human papillomavirus (hrHPV) among 7,126 asymptomatic women aged 30–49 years. The study found that 25.3% of participants tested positive for hrHPV, with infections outside the HPV16 and HPV18 groups being common. Among the 1,800 hrHPV-positive women, 1,422 had complete genotype data, revealing that HPV31/33/35/52/58 was the most frequently detected genotype (22.8%), followed by multiple genotypes (27.9%). Of the 1,121 hrHPV-positive women who underwent visual inspection with acetic acid (VIA), 159 (14.2%) had a positive inspection, and 8 (0.7%) were identified as having cervical cancer. However, the study found no statistically significant relationship between hrHPV genotypes and VIA outcomes. The authors emphasize the need for further research on the role of these genotypes in cervical carcinogenesis and the effectiveness of available vaccines against the broader range of hrHPV types observed.

Among the positive women with complete genotype data, infections outside the HPV16 and HPV18 groups were common, while a comparison of genotype groups with clinical assessment found no statistically significant relationship with visual inspection with acetic acid (VIA) outcome. The study focused on asymptomatic women who self-collected vaginal samples for testing with the GeneXpert HPV assay. The investigators recorded the presence or absence of specific hrHPV genotypes and used the results to describe genotype distribution in this rural population. All hrHPV-positive participants were invited to receive visual assessment for treatment, with or without VIA, and were treated according to the assessment. No histopathology was performed, so the clinical observations were not accompanied by a histological characterization of cervical lesions. Complete genotype information was available for 1,422 of the 1,800 hrHPV-positive participants. The largest individual genotype group was HPV31/33/35/52/58, detected in 324 women, or 22.8% of those with complete results. Multiple genotypes were the most frequent category, occurring in 397 women, or 27.9%. The remaining distribution consisted of HPV39/56/66/68 in 243 women, or 17.1%; HPV16 in 183, or 12.9%; HPV18/45 in 161, or 11.3%; and HPV51/59 in 114, or 8.0%. These figures show that the detected infections were not confined to HPV16 and HPV18 and that multiple hrHPV genotypes were frequently present. Discover more Book a Checkup Support Causes cancers Cancer Find Cancer Care Of the 1,121 hrHPV-positive women who underwent VIA, irrespective of genotype, 159, or 14.2%, had a positive inspection. Eight women, equal to 0.7% of those inspected, were identified as having cervical cancer. Six of these women were positive for HPV31/33/35/52/58, with three also positive for HPV16, HPV51/59, or HPV39/56/66/68. One woman was positive for HPV18/45, and one was HPV16-positive with an unknown additional genotype. The analysis found no statistically significant relationship between genotype and VIA outcome, meaning the study did not establish a statistical association between the genotype categories and the result of the inspection in the population examined. The findings support the authors’ conclusion that the study population had a high prevalence of non-16/18 hrHPV and that extended genotyping may be relevant to managing women at risk for cervical cancer in Uganda. The eight cervical cancers were identified within the smaller group of hrHPV-positive women who underwent VIA; the figures therefore describe findings in that clinical subgroup and should not be read as population-wide cervical-cancer incidence or direct estimates of genotype-specific risk. The authors call for additional research on the possible role of HPV31/33/35/52/58 in cervical carcinogenesis and on whether available vaccines protect the population against the broader range of hrHPV genotypes found in the study. HPV16 导致宫颈细胞学级别高的女性患癌症的风险最高 - Bioengineer.org

HPV16 Drives Highest Cancer Risk Among Women With High-Grade Cervical Cytology - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报Bioengineer.org 于 2026 年 10 月 27 日发表的一项研究分析了 2021 年 1 月至 2024 年 2 月在石家庄河北医科大学第二医院获得高级别宫颈细胞学结果的 511 名女性。研究发现,HPV16 是主要基因型,在 42.1% 的 队列研究 中检测到,并且与最高的癌症风险相关,其中 38.8% 的 HPV16 阳性女性患有 CIN3,41.7% 患有鳞状细胞癌。HPV16 阳性个体也承受着最重的癌前病变和恶性病变负担。由于数据缺失或其他因素,该研究排除了 181 名女性,留下 330 名女性进行分析。虽然研究结果表明 HPV16 和 宫颈癌 之间存在密切联系,但作者强调,这些结果只是假设生成,需要在更大的 队列研究 中进行验证。他们还指出了该检测的局限性,例如无法区分 HPV18 和 HPV45,并警告不要在没有进一步证据的情况下提出针对基因型的建议。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

HPV16 Drives Highest Cancer Risk Among Women With High-Grade Cervical Cytology - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study published on October 27, 2026, by Bioengineer.org analyzed 511 women with high-grade cervical cytology results from January 2021 to February 2024 at the Second Hospital of Hebei Medical University in Shijiazhuang. The research found that HPV16 was the dominant genotype, detected in 42.1% of the cohort, and was associated with the highest cancer risk, with 38.8% of HPV16-positive women having CIN3 and 41.7% having squamous cell carcinoma. HPV16-positive individuals also carried the heaviest burden of both precancerous and malignant lesions. The study excluded 181 women due to missing data or other factors, leaving 330 for analysis. While the findings suggest a strong link between HPV16 and cervical cancer, the authors emphasize that these results are hypothesis-generating and require validation in larger cohorts. They also note limitations in the assay, such as the inability to distinguish between HPV18 and HPV45, and caution against making genotype-specific recommendations without further evidence.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, October 30, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health HPV16 Drives Highest Cancer Risk Among Women With High-Grade Cervical Cytology by October 27, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 5 mins read 0 HPV16 Drives Highest Cancer Risk Among Women With High-Grade Cervical Cytology Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Women whose cervical screening shows high-grade abnormalities face sharply different risks depending on which high-risk human papillomavirus genotype is active in their cells, according to a retrospective study of 330 Chinese women published in Frontiers in Oncology. Among women with ASC-H or HSIL cytology, HPV16-positive individuals carried the heaviest burden of both precancerous lesions and invasive squamous cell carcinoma, while the two cases of adenocarcinoma in situ in the cohort both occurred in the small group positive for HPV18/45. Crucially, when the researchers adjusted for genotype, neither age nor menopausal status remained independently associated with the risk of cervical intraepithelial neoplasia grade 2 or worse, despite clear differences in the unadjusted data. The study, conducted at the Second Hospital of Hebei Medical University in Shijiazhuang, drew on 511 women recorded in the hospital’s ThinPrep cytology database between January 2021 and February 2024 with cytological results of atypical squamous cells, cannot exclude high-grade lesion, or high-grade squamous intraepithelial lesion. After excluding 181 women for reasons including missing Aptima HPV testing, absent biopsy or histopathological diagnosis, prior hysterectomy or malignancy, previous pelvic radiotherapy or chemotherapy, and autoimmune disease, 330 women aged 20 to 84 years remained for analysis. All had undergone the Aptima HPV assay, which detects transcriptionally active E6/E7 messenger RNA from 14 high-risk HPV types, followed by colposcopy-guided biopsy and histopathological examination interpreted by two experienced gynecological pathologists. Discover more Risk Uninterruptible power supplies Applied science journals The molecular distinction underlying the assay matters for interpreting the findings. Unlike DNA-based HPV tests, the Aptima assay detects E6/E7 mRNA, the transcripts of the viral oncogenes that drive malignant transformation, which the authors note offers higher specificity for clinically significant lesions. Positive specimens were further genotyped into three categories: HPV16, HPV18/45, and a pooled group of the other 11 high-risk types. Because the genotype assay cannot distinguish HPV18 from HPV45 or establish co-infection, the HPV18/45 category is inherently composite, a limitation that shaped some of the study’s most tentative conclusions. Discover more Find an OBGYN Book Lab Tests risk Get Risk Software The overall burden of disease in this high-risk cytology population was substantial. Histopathology revealed precancerous lesions, defined as CIN2, CIN3, or adenocarcinoma in situ, in 38.2 percent of the women, and malignant lesions, squamous cell carcinoma or adenocarcinoma, in 24.5 percent. Overall high-risk HPV positivity was 82.1 percent, rising from 70.4 percent among women with ASC-H cytology to 94.4 percent among those with HSIL cytology. The distribution of outcomes also differed significantly between the two cytology categories: 38.5 percent of HSIL cases harbored malignant lesions compared with 11.2 percent of ASC-H cases. Genotype stratification revealed a striking pattern. HPV16 was the dominant genotype, detected in 42.1 percent of the cohort, and HPV16-positive women showed the highest proportions of CIN3, at 38.8 percent, and squamous cell carcinoma, at 41.7 percent. Women positive for the other 11 high-risk types, who made up 36.1 percent of the sample, had a CIN2/3 prevalence of 44.5 percent, statistically comparable to the 43.9 percent seen in the HPV16 group. By contrast, among the 59 women who tested negative for high-risk HPV, benign findings predominated, with normal histology accounting for 59.3 percent of cases and a CIN2/3 prevalence of only 13.6 percent. The glandular findings, though suggestive, rest on very thin numbers. Only 13 women were HPV18/45-positive, and both adenocarcinoma in situ cases in the entire cohort occurred in this group, yielding a group-level prevalence of 15.4 percent that differed significantly from the other genotype groups. An exploratory multivariable analysis linked HPV18/45 positivity to AIS or adenocarcinoma with an odds ratio of 15.60, but the confidence interval stretched from 1.42 to 171.32, reflecting just two events. The authors explicitly frame this as hypothesis-generating and caution that it does not support genotype-specific diagnostic or interventional recommendations until confirmed in larger cohorts. Age and menopausal status told a more nuanced story. In unadjusted analyses, CIN2/3 prevalence fell steadily with advancing age, from 50.6 percent among women aged 25 to 34 to 12.5 percent among those 65 and older, while squamous cell carcinoma prevalence climbed from 8.6 percent to 58.3 percent across the same span. Premenopausal women had significantly higher CIN2/3 prevalence than postmenopausal women, 44.1 percent versus 21.3 percent, whereas postmenopausal women had far higher SCC prevalence, 45.7 percent versus 14.0 percent. Exploratory sensitivity analyses using alternative age cut-offs at 30, 40, 50, and 60 years showed broadly consistent patterns, though the authors note these P values were not adjusted for multiple comparisons. Discover more See an OB-GYN Find Cancer Care Cancer Support Causes Book a Checkup Yet when all three variables entered a multivariable logistic regression model simultaneously, the picture changed. HPV16 positivity carried an adjusted odds ratio of 23.79 for CIN2 or worse, HPV18/45 an odds ratio of 5.51, and the other 11 high-risk types an odds ratio of 4.26, all relative to HPV-negative women. Age, modeled as a continuous variable, and menopausal status showed no significant independent association. The authors report that variance inflation factors of 2.35 and 2.37 indicated no problematic multicollinearity between the correlated age and menopause variables, allowing both to be retained as prespecified covariates. Secondary models reinforced the genotype signal: younger age, HPV16, and the other high-risk types independently predicted CIN2/3, while increasing age and HPV16 independently predicted squamous cell carcinoma. One clinically consequential finding concerns the HPV-negative minority. Among the 59 women with high-grade cytology but negative Aptima results, histopathology still identified squamous HSIL or worse in 12 women, 20.3 percent, and one glandular lesion, for an overall prevalence of clinically significant disease of 22.0 percent. Among the nine HPV-negative women with HSIL cytology specifically, six, or 66.7 percent, were confirmed to have CIN2 or worse. The authors propose possible explanations, including inadequate sampling, E6/E7 mRNA expression below the assay’s detection threshold, infection with genotypes outside the assay’s target panel, or persistence of established lesions after viral clearance, but they emphasize that none of these mechanisms were directly evaluated and remain hypothetical. Their conclusion is that a negative HPV result must not override high-grade cytology, and that colposcopy with targeted biopsy remains mandatory for this population regardless of molecular status. The authors acknowledge the constraints of their design. The retrospective, single-center structure limits generalizability, and requiring both Aptima testing and histopathological confirmation may have preferentially selected women with more concerning clinical findings, a selection and verification bias that could inflate lesion prevalence. Excisional or surgical specimens were available for 47.0 percent of participants, and while the highest grade across all specimens defined the final diagnosis, reliance on biopsy alone in the remainder may have underestimated CIN2+ lesions. Centralized rereview of slides was not performed, leaving some interobserver variability unquantified. With these caveats in place, the study’s central message stands on firmer ground: in women whose cytology already signals high-grade disease, the active viral genotype, above all HPV16, refines immediate histopathological risk, while age and menopause, though visible in crude comparisons, add little once genotype is accounted for. 重组 HPV16 E7 疫苗 加上 CD137 激动剂可清除小鼠肿瘤 - Bioengineer.org

Recombinant HPV16 E7 vaccine plus CD137 agonism clears tumors in mice - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报Bioengineer.org 于 2026 年 10 月 27 日发表的一项研究报告称,重组 HPV16 E7 疫苗 与 CD137 激动剂联合使用时,在小鼠中表现出抗肿瘤活性。疫苗 被设计为具有重叠肽片段的单一重组蛋白,在 C57BL/6 小鼠中进行了测试,与野生型 E7 相比,它诱导了更强的细胞 免疫应答。在单独的 队列研究 中,ROP-E7 疫苗接种 比对照更有效地裂解肿瘤细胞。当与抗 PD-1 治疗 联合使用时,15 只小鼠中有 10 只实现了肿瘤完全消退,尽管单独使用 ROP-E7 治疗的所有 15 只小鼠最终都复发了。该研究强调了该平台治疗 HPV 疫苗接种 的潜力,但强调该结果是临床前的,需要在人体中进一步验证。该研究解决了现有治疗方法的局限性,例如野生型 E7 的安全性问题和基于表位的 疫苗 的 HLA 限制。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究发表于 2026 年 10 月 27 日。实验是在小鼠身上进行的,从第 5 天开始,每隔 14 天接种一次疫苗,并随访至第 98 天。
地点与对象
该研究是在实验室环境中使用 C57BL/6 小鼠作为实验群体进行的。
事件
由重组 HPV16 E7 癌蛋白组成的治疗剂 疫苗 与 CD137 激动剂相结合,在小鼠中进行了测试,并显示出抗肿瘤活性,当与抗 PD-1 治疗 组合时,一些小鼠实现了肿瘤完全消退。
规模、影响与风险
该研究报告称,用 ROP-E7 治疗的小鼠的肿瘤在数量上比用野生型 E7 治疗的小鼠要小,但差异并不具有统计学意义。与抗 PD-1 联合治疗时,15 只小鼠中有 10 只实现了肿瘤完全消退,而单独使用 ROP-E7 治疗的所有 15 只小鼠均出现肿瘤复发。ROP-E7 组的肿瘤进展风险降低,但该研究没有报告具体病例或死亡人数。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究正处于临床前阶段,结果显示对小鼠有良好的治疗效果。作者指出,这些发现基于体内实验,并且无法正式确定 ROP-E7 相对于野生型 E7 的优越性。该研究正在进行中,需要进一步的研究来在人类身上验证这些结果。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Recombinant HPV16 E7 vaccine plus CD137 agonism clears tumors in mice - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study published on October 27, 2026, by Bioengineer.org reports that a recombinant HPV16 E7 vaccine, when combined with CD137 agonism, showed antitumor activity in mice. The vaccine, designed as a single recombinant protein with overlapping peptide fragments, was tested in C57BL/6 mice, where it induced stronger cellular immune responses compared to wild-type E7. In a separate cohort, ROP-E7 vaccination led to more effective tumor cell lysis than controls. When combined with anti-PD-1 treatment, 10 out of 15 mice achieved complete tumor regression, though all 15 mice treated with ROP-E7 alone eventually relapsed. The study highlights the potential of this platform for therapeutic HPV vaccination but emphasizes that the results are preclinical and require further validation in humans. The research addresses the limitations of existing therapeutic approaches, such as safety concerns with wild-type E7 and HLA restrictions with epitope-based vaccines.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, October 30, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Technology Recombinant HPV16 E7 vaccine plus CD137 agonism clears tumors in mice by October 27, 2026 in Technology Reading Time: 5 mins read 0 Recombinant HPV16 E7 vaccine plus CD137 agonism clears tumors in mice Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A therapeutic vaccine engineered from the HPV16 E7 oncoprotein, delivered as a single recombinant protein rather than a mixture of synthetic peptides, has shown strong antitumor activity in mice, and its effect was substantially amplified when paired with an antibody that stimulates the co-stimulatory receptor 4-1BB, also known as CD137. The work, with Shisong Jiang as lead contact, offers a preclinical template for therapeutic HPV vaccination that avoids delivering intact, oncogenically active viral protein. The rationale for a new antigen format stems from a persistent gap in HPV medicine. Prophylactic vaccines targeting the L1 capsid protein generate neutralizing antibodies and prevent new infections, but they offer no therapeutic benefit to people who already carry established HPV-related lesions or cancers. HPV16 and HPV18 account for roughly 70 percent of cervical cancer cases, and persistent infection with high-risk HPV types remains the leading cause of cervical and other anogenital malignancies. No therapeutic vaccine has been approved for patients with premalignant lesions or HPV-positive tumors, although preclinical and clinical efforts targeting the viral oncogenic proteins E6 and E7 have shown promise, particularly those that harness cell-mediated immunity. Existing therapeutic approaches each carry drawbacks. DNA vaccines encoding the oncoproteins can activate cell-mediated immunity but raise safety concerns because E6 and E7 drive tumorigenesis. Wild-type protein vaccines cross-present antigen inefficiently, and epitope-based vaccines are restricted to patients with matching HLA types. Overlapping synthetic peptide and long synthetic peptide vaccines have achieved promising immunogenicity and clinical activity while using non-oncogenic peptide fragments, but peptide pools present practical problems: individual peptides are processed unevenly by antigen-presenting cells, some enter cells and access MHC molecules poorly, and synthesizing and quality-controlling many separate peptides adds regulatory complexity. The platform tested here, called a recombinant overlapping peptide or ROP vaccine, addresses those limitations by genetically concatenating overlapping peptide fragments spanning the full wild-type protein sequence into a single recombinant construct. For HPV16 E7, a 98-amino-acid oncoprotein, the team divided the sequence into 35-amino-acid fragments with 10-amino-acid overlaps and joined them with LRMK linker motifs. The 35-residue length was chosen because long peptides are efficiently internalized by professional antigen-presenting cells and require intracellular processing before presentation, while the 10-residue overlap exceeds the typical length of minimal CD8 T cell epitopes, ensuring that every potential epitope is retained in at least one fragment. The LRMK motif serves as a cleavage site for cathepsin S, a protease abundantly expressed in professional antigen-presenting cells, so cleavage within endosomal compartments releases fragments for MHC loading. Safety is built into the design. Wild-type E7 contains conserved regions critical for oncogenic activity: the N-terminal CR1 and CR2 domains, including the LXCXE motif that binds the retinoblastoma tumor suppressor family, and the C-terminal CR3 zinc-binding motif that supports structural stability and dimerization. These activities depend on the tertiary structure and intracellular expression of the full-length protein. Because ROP-E7 is delivered as an exogenous recombinant protein that is rapidly proteolyzed into short linear epitopes lacking higher-order structure, the functional domains required for pRb binding and zinc-dependent dimerization cannot be reconstituted. In silico analysis using the Immune Epitope Database suggested the construct may generate a broader array of potential T cell epitopes than wild-type E7, although the authors note that computational prediction does not directly assess epitope usage in vivo. Immunization experiments in C57BL/6 mice bore out the design intent. Three subcutaneous doses of MPLA-adjuvanted ROP-E7, given at 14-day intervals, induced significantly stronger cellular responses than the same regimen with wild-type E7, measured by interferon-gamma ELISPOT after ex vivo restimulation with purified WT-E7 protein. Flow cytometry showed higher proportions of activated CD4 and CD8 T cells, and MHC class I tetramer staining for the immunodominant H-2Db-restricted E7 epitope RAHYNIVTF revealed greater expansion of E7-specific CD8 T cells in the ROP-E7 group. In a separate cohort immunized at 7-day intervals, splenocytes from ROP-E7-vaccinated mice lysed HPV16 E7-expressing TC-1 tumor cells far more effectively than splenocytes from controls. Therapeutic efficacy was tested in the TC-1 model, in which mice were inoculated subcutaneously with 2 x 10^5 TC-1 cells on day 0 and vaccination began on day 5, when tumors were generally not yet palpable or only minimally palpable. The authors emphasize that this was an early-treatment setting rather than a model of large established tumors. Both vaccines inhibited tumor growth and prolonged survival relative to PBS and MPLA controls, with control tumors reaching roughly 1,500 cubic millimeters by day 22, WT-E7-treated tumors averaging about 700 cubic millimeters, and ROP-E7-treated tumors numerically smaller at under 500 cubic millimeters. The differences between the two vaccine groups did not reach statistical significance, so superiority of ROP-E7 over WT-E7 could not be formally established on these endpoints, though the trend prompted deeper analysis of the tumor immune microenvironment. That analysis combined bulk RNA sequencing with two immune deconvolution algorithms, mMCP-counter and Digital Cell Quantification, plus flow cytometry of tumor-infiltrating lymphocytes. Tumors from ROP-E7-treated mice carried stronger T cell and CD8 T cell transcriptional signatures and significantly more CD8 tetramer-positive T cells than tumors from WT-E7-vaccinated mice, while intratumoral regulatory T cells showed a non-significant trend toward reduction and splenic Treg frequencies were unchanged, indicating that immune modulation was localized to the tumor. DCQ analysis associated ROP-E7 with CD8 effector memory and memory signatures, whereas WT-E7 tumors showed relatively higher CD4 memory and Treg signatures, and PBS control tumors carried higher stromal signatures including endothelial cells and cancer-associated fibroblasts. Combination testing produced the study’s most striking results. Adding anti-PD-1 concurrently with the first vaccination did not improve tumor control over ROP-E7 alone under the dosing and schedule used, which the authors attribute to possible differences in antibody dose and timing relative to vaccine priming, since PD-1 signaling contributes to early CD8 T cell activation and clonal expansion. In contrast, combining ROP-E7 with a relatively low dose of 4-1BB agonistic antibody, which had limited activity as monotherapy, led to complete tumor eradication in the majority of treated mice within the initial 23-day analysis window and a trend toward prolonged survival. Extended follow-up to day 98 revealed the divergence clearly: all 15 ROP-E7 monotherapy mice eventually relapsed, yielding zero complete responses, while 10 of 15 combination-treated mice achieved complete regression and remained tumor-free. Transcriptomics showed the combination increased tumor-associated high endothelial venule signatures, structures that facilitate lymphocyte entry into tumors, and reduced Treg and exhaustion signatures, with tumor-infiltrating CD8 T cells expressing lower levels of PD-1 and LAG-3 than in the monotherapy group. Durability, however, proved incomplete. Four tumor-free combination-treated mice were rechallenged with TC-1 cells on day 98; by day 120 one remained tumor-free while three developed tumors, indicating that long-term immune memory was partially retained but not uniformly protective. A second course of the combination regimen given on day 123 cleared recurrent tumors in two of the three mice with established recurrences, and a single ROP-E7 booster restored strong ex vivo cytotoxic activity in splenocytes from tumor-rejecting mice, showing that functional memory persisted but required antigenic re-stimulation. The authors caution that all findings come from a murine model that does not reproduce the heterogeneity of human HPV-associated malignancies, that safety assessment of 4-1BB agonism was limited to the absence of significant body weight loss despite known dose-dependent hepatotoxicity concerns with systemic agonists, that epitope breadth was inferred computationally rather than mapped experimentally, and that the mechanistic contribution of the LRMK linkers remains to be directly demonstrated. Within those limits, the study supports ROP-based antigen engineering, and its pairing with co-stimulatory immunotherapy, as a candidate strategy for therapeutic HPV vaccination. View five news elements
Time
The study was published on October 27, 2026. The experiments were conducted with mice vaccinated at 14-day intervals starting on day 5, and follow-up extended to day 98.
Location and population
The research was conducted in a laboratory setting using C57BL/6 mice as the experimental population.
Event
A therapeutic vaccine composed of a recombinant HPV16 E7 oncoprotein, combined with CD137 agonism, was tested in mice and showed antitumor activity, with some mice achieving complete tumor regression when combined with anti-PD-1 treatment.
Scale, impact and risk
The study reported that tumors in mice treated with ROP-E7 were numerically smaller than those treated with wild-type E7, though the difference was not statistically significant. In combination with anti-PD-1, 10 out of 15 mice achieved complete tumor regression, while all 15 mice treated with ROP-E7 alone relapsed. The risk of tumor progression was reduced in the ROP-E7 group, but the study did not report specific case or death counts.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study is in the preclinical phase, with results showing promising therapeutic efficacy in mice. The authors note that the findings are based on in vivo experiments and that the superiority of ROP-E7 over wild-type E7 could not be formally established. The research is ongoing, and further studies are needed to validate these results in humans.

Original source text (Chinese)
新闻 体育 汽车 房产 旅游 教育 时尚 科技 财经 娱乐 除了HPV16、18,这些高危亚型同样值得警惕! 2026-10-27 15:55 来源: 孚瑞坦 发布于:福建省 在宫颈癌防控科普中,HPV16、18因与约70%的宫颈癌发病直接相关,始终是公众关注的重点。但高危型人乳头瘤病毒家族不只这2个,部分非HPV16、18型别的流行强度与致癌风险同样不容忽视,在亚洲女性群体中,部分亚型的感染占比甚至高于全球平均水平。除16、18型外,HPV31、33、45、52、58等是临床中检出率较高、致癌潜力明确的核心亚型。 其中,HPV52、58型是国内女性感染率排名靠前的高危亚型。国内多项流行病学调查显示,二者在宫颈病变人群中的检出占比显著高于欧美人群。这两种亚型均可诱导宫颈鳞状上皮细胞发生异型增生,持续感染状态下会推动病变从低级别向高级别进展,最终存在恶变可能,是我国宫颈癌防控中不可忽视的本土流行亚型。 HPV31、33、45型则是全球公认的高致癌风险亚型,三者合计贡献了10%~15%的宫颈癌病例。HPV31的致癌活性仅次于16、18型,可累及宫颈、阴道、外阴等多个部位;HPV33型与宫颈鳞癌、腺癌的发生均存在关联;HPV45型则在宫颈腺癌病例中占比相对突出。 此外,HPV35、39、51、56、59、68等亚型同样属于高危范畴,虽整体流行度偏低,但一旦形成持续感染,仍存在引发宫颈上皮内瘤变的潜在风险,筛查发现阳性后同样需要规范随访。 研究数据显示,80%以上的女性一生中会感染至少一种高危型HPV,其中多数为一过性感染,可通过自身免疫系统在1~2年内自行清除。仅有小部分的感染会发展为持续性感染,而高危HPV的持续感染正是宫颈上皮内瘤变(CIN)乃至宫颈癌发生的核心病因。从初次感染到病变进展通常有数年至十余年的时间窗,这一阶段也是无创干预、阻断病程的关键时期。 按照我国子宫颈癌三级规范化防治原则,及时清除高危HPV持续性感染、降低病毒载量,是避免病变进展、减少有创治疗的关键环节。针对高危HPV感染的干预,合规的二类医疗器械可作为重要的辅助手段。 孚瑞坦抗人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)凝胶敷料是经药品监督管理局批准的二类医疗器械(注册证号:桂械注准20232180013),获批适用范围为:阻断生殖道高危人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)感染,阻止病原微生物定植,用于降低高危人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)载量,防止宫颈病变发生;阻断人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)感染,阻止皮肤病变,降低物理治疗后尖锐湿疣复发率。 该产品采用IgY生物抗体靶向技术,通过抗HPV-L1-IgY、抗HPV-E6/E7-IgY与抗妇科炎症复合IgY形成抗体协同体系,搭配蛋白膜乳化均聚技术,以改性明胶为纳米载体,可作用于生殖道黏膜部位。 有一项随机对照研究纳入120例高危HPV感染合并CIN患者,结果显示,使用孚瑞坦抗HPV凝胶敷料治疗后,HPV转阴率可达88.33%,疗效优于对照组,同时可有效提升CIN病变转阴比例,相关研究成果已发表于专业医药期刊。[1] 宫颈癌防控离不开定期筛查与规范干预,重视所有高危亚型的感染风险,在合适的窗口期采取合规干预手段,才能更全面地守护女性生殖道健康。 品牌简介 孚瑞坦,专业健康品牌,深耕病毒领域。品牌强国医疗器械行业优选成员单位。现产品体系涵盖HPV、HSV等病毒防治需求。拥有以IgY(卵黄免疫球蛋白)为核心的多项专利,致力于用前沿生物科技,惠及全球亿万家庭。 温馨提示:请仔细阅读产品说明书或者在医务人员的指导下购买和使用。本文旨在健康科普,具体诊疗请遵医嘱。 参考文献: [1]苏玲.孚瑞坦抗人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)凝胶敷料治疗高危HPV感染合并CIN的临床疗效分析[J].中文科技期刊数据库(全文版)医药卫生,2024(5):0186-0189返回搜狐,查看更多 阅读 () 平台声明 该文观点仅代表作者本人,搜狐号系信息发布平台,搜狐仅提供信息存储空间服务。

X 新闻 体育 汽车 房产 旅游 教育 时尚 科技 财经 娱乐 母婴 健康 历史 军事 美食 更多 文化 星座 专题 游戏 搞笑 动漫 宠物 新闻 体育 汽车 房产 旅游 教育 时尚 科技 财经 娱乐 母婴 健康 历史 军事 美食 文化 星座 专题 游戏 搞笑 动漫 宠物 更多 无障碍 关怀版 登录 HPV18+6混合感染,一边高危一边长疣,该先处理哪个?苏利哲医生科学解答 2026-10-26 16:40 来源: 郑州美中商都医院 链接复制成功 发布于:河南省 查出HPV18高危型阳性,同时又长出了HPV6引发的尖锐湿疣,很多感染者瞬间陷入两难:有人觉得先把可见的疣体烧掉就行,高危型病毒不用急着管;也有人一门心思盯着高危型调理,放任疣体反复扩散,两种极端做法最后都把身体拖进了更被动的局面。郑州美中商都医院苏利哲主任深耕HPV诊疗领域多年,在临床中见过大量这类混合感染的患者,靠单一方向干预,最后反而出现疣体反复复发、高危型悄悄进展的情况。 29岁的张女士就是典型案例,查出HPV18+6混合感染时,外阴已经长出了好几颗明显的疣体,她一开始只想着先把疣体去掉,做了两次激光就觉得治疗完成,完全没管高危型HPV18的后续管理,不到半年疣体就再次复发,复查时还发现宫颈已经出现了CIN1低级别病变。苏利哲主任解释,很多人误以为混合感染要分先后顺序,要么先清疣要么先调高危,其实这是完全错误的思路:HPV6引发的疣体表面附着大量高活性病毒颗粒,持续刺激局部黏膜破损,会让HPV18更容易向宫颈深层上皮定植,加速病变进展;而HPV18的持续感染会不断削弱宫颈局部免疫,反过来又会让HPV6的疣体反复复发,两者互相推动,形成越治越麻烦的恶性循环。 苏利哲主任针对张女士的情况,制定了“同步分层干预”的方案:先精准清除可见疣体和潜伏的亚临床感染灶,同时同步调理宫颈局部免疫,阻断HPV18的持续定植,仅仅6个月后张女士复查,不仅疣体完全清除没有复发,HPV18和HPV6两个亚型也全部实现转阴,宫颈上的低级别病变也彻底逆转。苏利哲主任特别提醒,HPV18+6混合感染从来不存在“谁先谁后”的优先级,同步阻断疣体扩散和高危型病毒进展,才是避免两头出问题的最稳妥路径。返回搜狐,查看更多 X 新闻 体育 汽车 房产 旅游 教育 时尚 科技 财经 娱乐 母婴 健康 历史 军事 美食 更多 文化 星座 专题 游戏 搞笑 动漫 宠物 新闻 体育 汽车 房产 旅游 教育 时尚 科技 财经 娱乐 母婴 健康 历史 军事 美食 文化 星座 专题 游戏 搞笑 动漫 宠物 更多 无障碍 关怀版 登录 查出HPV18+CIN1,坚持4个月,为什么医生说别硬等?张振强主任权/威解答 2026-10-26 16:28 来源: 长沙中研医院HPV诊疗 链接复制成功 发布于:湖南省 HPV18型是和HPV16并列的最高危亚型,致病隐蔽性极强,很多人查出合并CIN1低级别病变后,第一反应就是回家硬等,觉得靠自身免疫力就能自然转阴,拖到半年一年再复查也没关系,这恰恰是HPV18感染后最危险的认知误区。 长沙中研皮肤病医院HPV科张振强主任深耕HPV诊疗领域20余年,在临床中见过大量HPV18阳性合并CIN1的患者,硬等半年后复查,病变非但没有消退,反而悄悄进展,甚至直接跳级到CIN2、CIN3的案例。他解释,普通低危型或其他高危亚型引发的CIN1,自然转阴概率确实偏高,但HPV18型的病毒载量上升速度快、对宫颈深层黏膜的侵袭力强,哪怕只是合并低级别病变,也不能照搬“保守观察1年以上”的通用标准,盲目硬等很可能错过最佳干预窗口。 很多患者觉得“CIN1是最轻的病变,根本不用处理”,但张振强主任提醒,HPV18型感染引发的CIN1,有相当一部分病灶藏在宫颈管深处,常规TCT和普通阴道镜很难完全发现,硬等的过程中病毒会持续破坏局部黏膜免疫,原本可以轻松逆转的病变,很可能在几个月内悄悄进展。他接诊过一位27岁的患者,查出HPV18+CIN1后坚持硬等8个月,复查时直接发现颈管内隐匿的高级别病变,原本无需手术的情况,最后不得不做锥切干预。 针对这类患者,张振强主任不会直接建议手术,也不会放任患者盲目硬等,而是采用“精准排查+局部免疫激活”的分层干预方案,先通过精细化检查把藏在褶皱里的隐匿病灶全部找出来,再用中西医结合的方式快速提升宫颈局部的抗病毒能力,很多坚持规范干预4个月的患者,复查时不仅HPV18成功转阴,CIN1病变也完全逆转,不用承受不必要的手术创伤。他特别强调,HPV18合并CIN1从来不是“等等就好”的问题,科学干预远比重度拖延更稳妥。返回搜狐,查看更多 首赞 +1 点赞失败 阅读 () 作者声明 个人观点,仅供参考 平台声明 该文观点仅代表作者本人,搜狐号系信息发布平台,搜狐仅提供信息存储空间服务。 内容举报 by Taboolaby Taboola Sponsored LinksSponsored Links Promoted LinksPromoted Links 你可能会喜欢 A Christmas Train Display Everyone Will Want to Seechristmas Undo Meet The Cat Advent Calendar With A Surprise Every DayGIFTS Undo This Japanese Sharpener Restores Razor-Like Sharpness to Any KnifeSherum Undo Here's The Estimated Walk-In Shower Price in 2026Homebuddy Undo Urologists: 87% of men don't know about this easy trick (start tonight)The Mens Report Undo The Christmas Village Skating Town Breaks All Sales Records in USchristmas Undo 推荐阅读 闽超决赛即将上演,厦门白鹭体育场,终于等来“闽超”的巅峰对决 精选所见所闻 · 10-15 03:16 今晚19:00!全胜厦门队VS翻盘福州队,闽超终极一战,冠军归谁? B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

通用 HPV 疫苗接种 和肛门癌筛查成为消除 HPV 相关癌症的中心舞台 - Bioengineer.org

Universal HPV vaccination and anal cancer screening take centre stage in push to eliminate HPV-associated cancers - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报The article discusses the push to eliminate HPV-associated cancers by expanding prevention strategies beyond cervical cancer in women. Experts at the 18th technical meeting of the HPV Prevention and Control Board in Antwerp, Belgium, in June 2025 concluded that universal HPV vaccination, including boys, and targeted anal cancer screening for high-risk populations are essential. The report highlights that HPV causes approximately 148,072 cancer cases in men annually, with oropharyngeal cancer being the most common. Data from Finland shows that universal vaccination significantly reduces HPV16/18 prevalence, and the WHO-recommended single-dose schedule has been adopted, with 28 million girls and 6 million boys aged 9 to 14 receiving a first dose in 2023. However, implementation remains uneven, and coverage gaps persist, particularly in low- and middle-income countries. The report also notes that anal cancer screening is the most advanced non-cervical strategy, but its implementation is constrained. The analysis is based on epidemiological data, modeling evidence, and country experiences, with the goal of achieving broader public health benefits and reducing the incidence gap between different income levels.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章发表于2026年10月28日。讨论的事件包括2025年6月举行的HPV预防控制委员会第18次技术会议的数据。
地点与对象
会议在比利时安特卫普举行。分析涵盖全球人口,特别提到北美、欧洲和大洋洲等高收入国家和地区。它还强调了面临风险的人群,包括男男性行为者(MSM),特别是艾滋病毒感染者,以及各国的女孩和男孩。
事件
HPV 预防和控制委员会召集的专家得出的结论是,消除 HPV 相关癌症需要将女性的预防范围扩大到 宫颈癌 以外的范围,并将包括男孩在内的通用 HPV 疫苗接种 与针对高危人群的有针对性的肛门癌筛查相结合。这是基于流行病学数据、模型证据和国家经验。
规模、影响与风险
HPV 每年在全球范围内导致约 148,072 例男性癌症病例,保守估计每年约为 180,000 例。这些癌症主要影响口咽、肛门和阴茎。口咽癌是男性中最常见的 HPV 相关恶性肿瘤,高收入国家的 发病率 发病率在过去 15 年中增加了一倍多。报告还指出,普遍的 发病率 战略可以缩小低收入和中等收入国家与高收入国家之间的 发病率 差距。
应对、状态与不确定性
The meeting discussed the need for universal HPV vaccination and anal cancer screening. A community-randomised trial in Finland showed that universal vaccination significantly reduced HPV16/18 prevalence. The WHO-recommended single-dose schedule has been adopted, with 28 million girls and 6 million boys aged 9 to 14 receiving a first dose in 2023. However, implementation remains uneven, and supply dynamics are shifting. The report also highlights equity gaps in coverage, such as in Scotland and Cameroon.

The article discusses the push to eliminate HPV-associated cancers by expanding prevention strategies beyond cervical cancer in women. Experts at the 18th technical meeting of the HPV Prevention and Control Board in Antwerp, Belgium, in June 2025 concluded that universal HPV vaccination, including boys, and targeted anal cancer screening for high-risk populations are essential. HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, October 30, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Universal HPV vaccination and anal cancer screening take centre stage in push to eliminate HPV-associated cancers by October 28, 2026 in Biology, Health Reading Time: 5 mins read 0 Universal HPV vaccination and anal cancer screening take centre stage in push to eliminate HPV-associated cancers Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Experts convened by the HPV Prevention and Control Board at its 18th technical meeting in Antwerp, Belgium, in June 2025 concluded that eliminating HPV-associated cancers will require extending prevention beyond the traditional focus on cervical cancer in women, combining universal HPV vaccination that includes boys with targeted anal cancer screening for the populations at highest risk. The meeting report, published in BMC Proceedings, synthesises the epidemiology, modelling evidence and country experiences discussed at the meeting. The case for including men rests on a substantial and growing burden of disease. HPV causes approximately 148,072 cancer cases in men annually worldwide, a figure the meeting’s participants described as likely a substantial undercount, with conservative re-estimates suggesting around 180,000 cases per year. These cancers affect primarily the oropharynx, anus and penis. Oropharyngeal cancer is the most common HPV-associated malignancy in men, and incidence among males in high-income countries has more than doubled over the past 15 years, now exceeding that of cervical cancer in women; men are five times more likely to be diagnosed than women. Age-standardised incidence rates per 100,000 are highest in North America at 6.0, Europe at 5.5 and Oceania at 5.3. Discover more Book Lab Tests Book Care Services Vaccine Biological differences between sexes reinforce the argument for vaccinating boys. Men have lower seroconversion rates than women following natural genital HPV infection, suggesting prolonged susceptibility, and unlike in women, in whom HPV prevalence declines with age, oral and genital HPV prevalence in men remains relatively constant across age groups. Data from 64 studies covering approximately 29,900 men show wide variability in anal high-risk HPV prevalence by risk group: 74.3 percent among gay, bisexual and other men who have sex with men living with HIV, 41.2 percent among HIV-negative men who have sex with men, and 6.9 percent in HIV-negative men who have sex with women. Discover more Find Cancer Care Cancer cancer cancers Direct trial evidence supports the superiority of universal over girls-only vaccination. Between 2007 and 2014, a community-randomised trial across 33 Finnish communities enrolled over 80,000 adolescents, randomising them to universal HPV16/18 vaccination, girls-only HPV16/18 vaccination or control hepatitis B vaccination, with overall coverage of 52 percent in girls and 29 percent in boys. In communities where both boys and girls were vaccinated, HPV16/18 prevalence was significantly reduced not only among vaccinated but also among unvaccinated females, and the herd effect was stronger in the universal arms than in girls-only arms. Prevalence of other high-risk types, including HPV18, 31, 33 and 35, was also markedly lower in the universal vaccination group. By contrast, girls-only and lower-coverage arms offered limited herd protection, leaving unvaccinated and marginalised women disproportionately affected. The meeting heard that even moderate-coverage universal vaccination can deliver substantial public health benefits within five years, and that modelling and ecological observations suggest elimination of the most oncogenic types, HPV16 and 18, may be feasible within 15 to 30 years if universal strategies are widely adopted. Modelling analyses nonetheless identify high coverage in girls as the most efficient strategy for cervical cancer elimination specifically. The HPV-ADVISE transmission-dynamic model, calibrated to six core countries and mapped to 67 low- and middle-income countries and 40 high-income countries, found routine single-dose vaccination of 9-year-old girls to be the most efficient strategy, with adding boys offering limited additional benefit because of herd immunity conferred by high coverage in girls. Increasing coverage in girls from 40 to 80 percent yielded up to three times the reduction in cervical cancer cases compared with initiating vaccination in boys at the same coverage level. However, meeting participants stressed that cost-effectiveness is necessary but not sufficient for policy decisions: when equity, acceptability, programme resilience and the prevention of non-cervical cancers are incorporated, universal vaccination is justified on broader public health grounds, including reducing the projected incidence gap between low- and middle-income and high-income countries. Discover more Book a Checkup Find Vaccines Check Symptoms Global implementation remains uneven. By 2025, 149 of 194 countries had introduced HPV vaccine into national immunisation schedules, but 55 percent of girls worldwide still live in countries without an HPV vaccination programme. Average first-dose coverage rose modestly from 55 percent in 2021 to 62 percent in 2023, and only 16 countries achieved greater than 90 percent first-dose national coverage in 2023. The adoption of the WHO-recommended single-dose schedule has accelerated scale-up, with approximately 28 million girls and 6 million boys aged 9 to 14 receiving a first dose in 2023. Supply dynamics are shifting, with new manufacturers and technology transfers reshaping the market, and supply projected to stabilise at around 100 million doses annually, potentially peaking at 130 million under universal vaccination. Country experiences presented at the meeting highlighted persistent equity gaps. In Scotland, where the school-based programme was introduced for girls in 2008 and extended to boys in 2019, uptake remains uneven: among pupils in the fourth year of secondary school in 2024/25, coverage was 75 percent in the most deprived areas compared with 91 percent in the least deprived, a gap that has increased substantially since the programme was extended to boys. In Cameroon, early pilot programmes demonstrated strong community engagement, with 74 percent of girls in Foumban and Edéa completing both doses, but the 2020 national rollout faced strong resistance from religious leaders and some healthcare professionals, and two years after launch 84 percent of health facilities were not offering HPV vaccination. A revamped strategy combining a single-dose schedule, extension to boys, periodic intensification of routine immunisation and renewed community partnerships improved coverage across all regions, with four regions reaching over 90 percent coverage in girls and around 40 percent in boys, although the report notes that the concurrent interventions make it difficult to attribute coverage gains to any single measure. On secondary prevention, the meeting identified anal cancer screening as the most advanced non-cervical strategy warranting immediate attention. The pivotal ANCHOR study, a randomised controlled trial involving more than 10,000 individuals with HIV, demonstrated that treating biopsy-proven anal high-grade squamous intraepithelial lesions through office-based electrocautery reduced progression to anal cancer by 57 percent compared with active monitoring. Notably, 53 percent of men, 47 percent of women and 67 percent of transgender individuals screened were diagnosed with high-grade lesions, highlighting the high prevalence of precancerous lesions in these populations. In the monitoring arm, progression from high-grade lesions to cancer occurred at a rate of 400 per 100,000 person-years, and the risk of progression was 5.26-fold higher for lesions involving more than 50 percent of the anal or perianal canal compared with smaller lesions, providing an early basis for lesion-based risk stratification. Implementation of anal screening remains constrained despite the publication in 2024 of the first evidence-based guidelines from the U.S. Health and Human Services and the International Anal Neoplasia Society. Barriers include limited high-resolution anoscopy capacity, workforce shortages, stigma, and limited evidence on optimal screening algorithms, with all current screening recommendations based on expert opinion rather than prospective evidence. In the United States, only 22 percent of people in HIV care received care at facilities with on-site high-resolution anoscopy, and one-third attended clinics with no known access to such services. Experiences from Puerto Rico, where over 1,400 patients have been evaluated at the Anal Neoplasia Clinic of the University of Puerto Rico Comprehensive Cancer Centre since 2014, and from Belgium, where an estimated 9,500 people living with HIV aged over 50 and up to 235,000 men who have sex with men and transwomen over 45 may require regular screening, illustrate both the growing demand and the bottlenecks in trained personnel and coordination. The meeting concluded that integrating universal vaccination with targeted anal cancer screening, supported by equitable access, biomarker development, self-sampling approaches and living guidelines that evolve with emerging evidence, is essential to reducing the HPV-related cancer burden in both women and men. Participants emphasised that vaccinating boys will not protect hard-to-reach girls, since people often partner within their ethnic or social circles where similar barriers to vaccination exist, and that sustained investment in communication, community engagement and workforce training will be required to translate these strategies into population-level impact. Time
The article was published on October 28, 2026. The event discussed includes data from the 18th technical meeting of the HPV Prevention and Control Board held in June 2025.
Location and population
The meeting took place in Antwerp, Belgium. The analysis covers global populations, with specific mention of high-income countries and regions such as North America, Europe, and Oceania. It also highlights populations at risk, including men who have sex with men (MSM), particularly those living with HIV, and girls and boys in various countries.
Event
Experts convened by the HPV Prevention and Control Board concluded that eliminating HPV-associated cancers requires extending prevention beyond cervical cancer in women, combining universal HPV vaccination that includes boys with targeted anal cancer screening for high-risk populations. This is based on epidemiological data, modeling evidence, and country experiences.
Scale, impact and risk
HPV causes approximately 148,072 cancer cases in men annually worldwide, with conservative re-estimates suggesting around 180,000 cases per year. These cancers primarily affect the oropharynx, anus, and penis. Oropharyngeal cancer is the most common HPV-associated malignancy in men, with incidence rates in high-income countries more than doubling over the past 15 years. The report also notes that universal vaccination strategies could reduce the incidence gap between low- and middle-income countries and high-income countries.
Response, status and uncertainty
The meeting discussed the need for universal HPV vaccination and anal cancer screening. A community-randomised trial in Finland showed that universal vaccination significantly reduced HPV16/18 prevalence. The WHO-recommended single-dose schedule has been adopted, with 28 million girls and 6 million boys aged 9 to 14 receiving a first dose in 2023. However, implementation remains uneven, and supply dynamics are shifting. The report also highlights equity gaps in coverage, such as in Scotland and Cameroon.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

South Africa’s HPV Vaccination Drive to Protect Girls from Cervical Cancer is Working – What We Know After 10 Years - Angels in Medicine

News Brief
South Africa’s school-based HPV vaccination programme, introduced in 2014, has demonstrated measurable benefits in reducing infections with high-risk HPV types, particularly HPV 16 and 18, among adolescent girls. A study conducted in 2019 and 2023 found that these infections fell by 83% in vaccinated groups and 82% in girls living with HIV. The programme's success is attributed to its impact on preventing cervical cancer, which remains a major health issue in the country. The findings suggest that the programme is effective in reducing the risk of cervical cancer, even in high-risk populations. Continued monitoring is essential to ensure long-term protection, especially for those living with HIV. The study highlights the importance of HPV vaccination in public health and serves as a model for other countries facing similar challenges.
Original source text (English)
South Africa’s school-based HPV vaccination programme is delivering measurable population-level benefits. By The Conversation First published October 19, 2026 by The Conversation and reprinted October 22, 2026 by Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance/VaccinesWork South Africa introduced a vaccination for human papillomavirus (HPV) in public schools in 2014. The programme offered the vaccination to grade 4 girls aged nine years and older. Uptake was high from the start. About 87% of eligible girls received at least one dose during the first year. Almost all cervical cancers are caused by persistent infection with HPV, a very common virus transmitted through sexual contact. Most HPV infections clear naturally, but some high-risk types can persist and can eventually lead to cervical cancer. HPV types 16 and 18 alone are responsible for around 70% of cervical cancer cases worldwide. Cervical cancer is one of the most preventable forms of cancer, yet it remains a major cause of illness and death among women worldwide. Each year, more than 660,000 women are diagnosed globally, and over 90% of new cases and deaths caused by cervical cancer happen in low- and middle-income countries. The challenge is particularly acute in South Africa because HIV increases the risk of HPV infection persisting and progressing to cervical disease. Women living with HIV are much more likely to develop cervical cancer during their lifetime than women without HIV. South Africa had an estimated 7.9 million people living with HIV in mid-2025, accounting for 12.3% of the total population. We are public health researchers dedicated to advancing prevention and treatment of sexually transmitted infections. We evaluated the 2014 vaccination programme’s impact on HPV prevalence among adolescent girls in a setting of high HIV prevalence in South Africa. Our findings were striking. They showed that: - infections with HPV types 16 and 18 fell by 83% between pre-vaccine and post-vaccine groups - infections with the HPV types responsible for most cervical cancers have fallen by more than 80% among adolescent girls eligible for vaccination, including those living with HIV. This is encouraging news for a country that carries one of the world’s highest burdens of cervical cancer. It also provides important evidence for other African countries with high HIV prevalence that are working towards eliminating cervical cancer as a public health problem. Measuring the impact Because cervical cancer can take decades to develop after HPV infection, it is still too early to measure the programme’s success by looking at cancer rates alone. Instead, our research examined whether vaccine-targeted HPV infections are becoming less common among vaccine-programme eligible generations. A substantial reduction in these infections is a strong early sign that future cervical cancer rates will fall. To assess the programme’s impact, we conducted the HPV One and Two Dose Population Effectiveness (HOPE) study in 2019 and 2023. We compared how common HPV was among adolescent girls aged 17-18 years who were too old to receive the HPV vaccine when the national programme began, with girls of the same age who had been eligible for vaccination through the school-based programme. The study included 2,357 participants recruited from public healthcare facilities across four South African provinces. Importantly, about one-quarter of participants were living with HIV. This allowed us to examine vaccine impact in a population that faces a higher risk of cervical cancer. Our findings Across all participants, infections with HPV types 16 and 18 fell by 83% between pre-vaccine and post-vaccine groups. Before the vaccination programme, about one in five girls carried one of these high-risk HPV types. Among girls eligible for vaccination, that number fell to approximately three in every 100 girls. The programme performed equally well among girls living with HIV. In this group, HPV 16/18 infections fell from 29% to 4%, representing an 82% reduction. We also observed reductions in high-risk HPV types that are genetically related to HPV 16 and 18, a phenomenon known as cross-protection. This increased the overall impact of the vaccination programme. At the same time, other HPV types did not fall. This strengthens confidence that the observed reductions were due to vaccination, not other factors such as changes in sexual behaviour. Another important finding was evidence of indirect protection also known as “herd protection”. Girls in the post-vaccine era who had no documented record of being vaccinated, or did not report having been vaccinated, had much lower rates of HPV 16 and 18 than girls in the pre-vaccine group. This suggests that when most people are vaccinated, the virus spreads much less in the wider community, helping protect unvaccinated individuals. What this means for South Africa and the region The findings provide compelling evidence that South Africa’s HPV vaccination programme is achieving its primary goal: preventing infections that would otherwise place young women at risk of developing cervical cancer later in life. They also demonstrate that the benefits extend to adolescent girls living with HIV, a group that stands to gain substantially from effective cervical cancer prevention. This is especially important for countries in eastern and southern Africa, where both HIV and cervical cancer remain major public health challenges. In 2022, the World Health Organization recommended a single-dose HPV vaccine for the general population, with the aim of simplifying delivery and increasing global coverage for settings that need it most. Since October 2024, South Africa has adopted a single-dose HPV vaccination approach. Continued monitoring will be important to ensure long-term protection, particularly among people living with HIV. A decade after its introduction, the country’s school-based HPV vaccination programme is delivering measurable population-level benefits and providing a model for other countries seeking to prevent one of the world’s most avoidable cancers. Website This article is republished from The Conversation under a Creative Commons license. Read the original article. Authors Dorothy Nyemba-Machemedze, Data Analyst – Epidemiologist/Biostatistician at Wits RHI, University of the Witwatersrand and Sinead Delany-Moretlwe, Associate Professor and Director: Research at the Wits Reproductive Health and HIV Institute I, University of the Witwatersrand Related Articles England’s NHS Rolls out Discreet Cervical Cancer Home-Testing Kits for Millions of Women Nearly four million women who’ve missed screening invitations will be offered free HPV self-testing swabs to complete at home, part of NHS England’s push to increase screening and eliminate cervical cancer entirely by 2040. Fewer Young Women Are Dying of Cervical Cancer in England A landmark study found no cervical cancer deaths among women aged 20 to 24 in England from 2020 to 2024, the first direct evidence that HPV vaccination saves lives, not just prevents infections. Researchers estimate it has already prevented roughly 200 deaths. Student Champions in Mozambique Promote HPV Vaccination in the Fight to Prevent Cervical Cancer In Mozambique, VillageReach’s INTEGRATE HPV initiative is reshaping cervical cancer prevention. When trained teachers and student peer champions led the conversation, fear gave way to enthusiasm. School-based HPV vaccinations surged nearly tenfold across intervention sites

The HPV vaccination programme was introduced in 2014, and the study was conducted in 2019 and 2023. The findings were reported in 2026.

South Africa’s school-based HPV vaccination programme has shown measurable population-level benefits in reducing infections with high-risk HPV types, particularly HPV 16 and 18, among adolescent girls, including those living with HIV. The study was conducted by public health researchers.
Scale, impact and risk
HPV 16 and 18 infections fell by 83% among vaccinated girls and 82% among girls living with HIV. These reductions are significant and suggest a strong impact on preventing cervical cancer. The programme's success is highlighted as a model for other countries.
Response, status and uncertainty

HPV 预防、地方行动推动菲律宾实现无 宫颈癌

HPV prevention, local action drive push for a cervical cancer-free Philippines
新闻简报文章重点介绍了菲律宾通过 HPV 预防消除 宫颈癌 的努力,重点关注当地行动和实施。它强调了 HPV 疫苗接种 在预防 宫颈癌 方面的重要性,并指出约 95% 的病例与持续性 高危型HPV 感染有关。世卫组织的 90-70-90 战略目标是到 15 岁实现 90% 的 疫苗接种 覆盖率,对 35-45 岁的女性实现 70% 的筛查,对确诊患者实现 90% 的 治疗 覆盖率。曼达维市等地方政府正在努力将 疫苗接种 扩展到私立学校,并从私人从业者那里收集数据,以解决覆盖范围的差距。卫生专家强调,当地需要持续努力,包括将消除 宫颈癌 纳入年度计划并通过多种来源获得资金。该文章强调了社区参与、意识和合作的重要性,以确保公共和私营部门的预防措施可及且有效。

重点是菲律宾,特别是地方政府 (LGU) 和社区,包括北伊罗戈省的版纳和奎松市的桑帕洛克等特定地区。受影响的人群包括女孩和妇女,特别是 9 至 14 岁的女孩和妇女,以及更广泛的社区。
